1:#!/usr/bin/env bash
2:#
3:# precompact_priorities.sh
4:#
5:# Claude Code fires PreCompact before each /compact (manual) and each
6:# auto compaction. This script's trimmed stdout becomes the
7:# "Additional Instructions:" block appended after the default 9-section
8:# compact prompt.
9:#
10:# Each rule below is a fidelity requirement layered onto one of the
11:# existing default sections (1-9) rather than a parallel taxonomy, so
12:# the summarizer has one coherent shape to fill.
13:#
14:# The quoted heredoc delimiter ('PRIORITY_BLOCK') disables every form of
15:# shell expansion, so apostrophes, em-dashes, and backticks in the block
16:# pass through untouched with zero escaping.
17:
18:cat << 'PRIORITY_BLOCK'
19:<priority-preservation-instructions>
20:These requirements augment the 9 required sections. They do not replace
21:any section — they raise the fidelity bar for content categories that
22:the default prompt leaves under-specified.
23:
24:A. UNANSWERED QUESTIONS (patches §6 and §7)
25:   The default §6 says to list ALL user messages but does not ask you
26:   to flag which ones went unanswered. For each §6 message, mark it as
27:   answered, partially answered, or unanswered. In §7, add a
28:   sub-heading "Pending Questions" at the top and list every
29:   unanswered or partially answered user question verbatim.
30:
31:B. ROOT CAUSES, NOT SYMPTOMS (patches §4 and §5)
32:   The default §4 asks for "errors and fixes" but does not distinguish
33:   confirmed root causes from ruled-out hypotheses. In §5, record
34:   every confirmed root cause with its file path and line number
35:   (pattern: `path/to/file.py:42`). In §4, keep ruled-out hypotheses
36:   so they don't get re-tried. Never paraphrase an error message,
37:   error code, or stack frame — preserve them verbatim.
38:
39:C. EXACT NUMBERS AND IDS (patches §3, §4, §5)
40:   The default prompt nowhere tells you to preserve exact digits. Do
41:   so everywhere they appear: benchmark results, profiling output,
42:   error rates, latencies, token counts, costs, PR numbers, issue
43:   numbers, commit SHAs, run IDs, dataset names, and model IDs. Never
44:   round, never paraphrase a quantitative value.
45:
46:D. FILE PATH IMPORTANCE TIERS (patches §3)
47:   The default §3 lists files flat. Group them by importance instead:
48:   critical (caused or fixed the issue), referenced (read for
49:   context), mentioned (appeared in discussion only). Use the pattern
50:   `path/to/file.py:42` whenever a specific line matters.
51:
52:E. SUBAGENT FINDINGS ARE PRIMARY EVIDENCE (patches §3 and §5)
53:   The default prompt does not call out Task / Agent tool results.
54:   For every such tool result in the transcript, preserve the agent's
55:   final report in full in §3 or §5 as appropriate — file paths, code
56:   references, citations, and quantitative findings it returned.
57:   Subagent runs are expensive to redo; treat their reports as
58:   primary evidence, not as compressible chatter.
59:
60:F. A-VS-B COMPARISONS (patches §1)
61:   The default §1 asks for explicit requests and intents but says
62:   nothing about alternatives under evaluation. When the user was
63:   weighing option A vs option B (tool X vs tool Y, approach 1 vs
64:   approach 2), preserve both sides and the decision criteria. If a
65:   decision was reached, record which side won and the reasoning.
66:
67:Priority when cutting for length: if A–F would otherwise be dropped
68:to fit within a section's length, drop conversational filler,
69:repeated tool output, and intermediate reasoning first.
70:</priority-preservation-instructions>
71:PRIORITY_BLOCK

13:#   TIER-1-DIRECT @ repomix README pack-grep workflow at Z:/repos/deps/repomix/README.md:979 + :1089 @ HEAD 7dfd2b96 (tree-sitter compression ~70% token reduction),

19:# Sister-rule integration:
20:# - karpathy-adapted.md §5 Wiki Compounding Surface (Layer 1 JSONL / Layer 2 index / Layer 3 compiled wiki)
21:# - coordination.md §12 rewind-first
22:# - team-orch-patterns.md §Context Budget per agent + §1M context calibration (rot threshold ~300-400k on Opus 4.7)
23:# - synthesis-layer-verify.md (orchestrator-side OVER/UNDER/HNF on agent returns)
24:# - codex-t1-fix-forward-pattern.md §Pattern B (HNF disposition when codex T1 timeout; ranking from this fire's [B] partial trace)
25:
26:## The rule
27:
28:When working in long-arc /loop sessions (≥4h wall-clock OR multi-Bundle ship cycles OR cron-driven autonomous arcs), context bloat dominates token cost. The CC-runtime autocompact at ~80% threshold fires blind and produces lossy summaries (per `karpathy-adapted.md §5`). SOTA auto-compact discipline is **pre-emptive, ranked, and routes-around the autocompact mechanism entirely**.
29:
30:## Ranked recipe (codex T1 ranking from /goal P2[B] partial trace 2026-05-13)
31:
32:### Rank #1 — context-mode `ctx_batch_execute` (PRIMARY, ~98% savings)
33:
34:**When**: any command/probe/script producing >20 lines of output. Multi-command batches with multiple search queries.
35:
36:**How**:
37:```
38:mcp__plugin_context-mode_context-mode__ctx_batch_execute(
39:  commands: [{label, command}, ...],
40:  queries: [<5-8 specific questions>],
41:  concurrency: 4-8 for I/O-bound batches
42:)
43:```
44:
45:Raw stdout/output indexed into FTS5 in subprocess sandbox; only the search-result excerpts enter context. **Single batch_execute call replaces 30+ ctx_execute + 10+ ctx_search calls**.
46:
47:**Sub-variants**:
48:- `ctx_execute(language: shell|python|javascript, code, intent: "...")` — single command with intent → indexed if output >5KB, returns titled previews
49:- `ctx_execute_file(path, language, code)` — read+process file content in sandbox; only printed result enters context
50:- `ctx_fetch_and_index(requests: [{url, source}], concurrency: 4-8)` — replaces WebFetch; raw HTML stays in sandbox, ~3KB preview + searchable knowledge base
51:
52:### Rank #2 — repomix pack→grep workflow (~70% compression for repo-scope audits)
53:
54:**When**: line-by-line auditing a `Z:/repos/deps/<repo>` subtree spanning ≥5 files. Replaces 10+ Read calls.
55:
56:**How** (per `Z:/repos/deps/repomix/src/mcp/mcpServer.ts:20-23 @ HEAD 7dfd2b96`):
57:1. **Pack**: `mcp__repomix__pack_codebase(directory, compress=true)` → returns `outputId`. Tree-sitter compression ~70% per `Z:/repos/deps/repomix/README.md:1089 @ 7dfd2b96`.
58:2. **Grep**: `mcp__repomix__grep_repomix_output(outputId, pattern, contextLines=5)` for incremental retrieval — preferred over re-packing uncompressed per README:979.
59:3. **Skill persist** (if learning is durably reusable): **DO NOT** use `mcp__repomix__generate_skill(directory, skillName)` for routine auto-compact reuse — that primitive writes `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` outside the documented install/provenance path, violating cardinal-rule-5 install-priority + cardinal-rule-6 official-native-channel. Instead: (a) persist topic-scoped findings to `.claude/projects/*/memory/` per Karpathy §5 Layer-3 compiled wiki (no install-class artifact created), OR (b) escalate as install-class candidate via sota-researcher dispatch with convergence-gate Axis-1+2+3 verification + Section 14 marketplace install path + `docs/install-provenance.md` row. Local `generate_skill` invocations bypass T1+T2 audit and provenance log.
60:
61:### Rank #3 — Pre-emptive `/compact <hint>` (superpowers + Karpathy §5 layered)
62:
63:**When**: context approaches 300k tokens (rot threshold on Opus 4.7 per Thariq 2026-04-16 at `coordination.md §12 Rewind-first` row Mid-task hint). **NEVER let CC autocompact fire blind at 80%**.
64:
65:**How**: invoke `/compact` with a focused hint that steers the lossy summary toward retained-value content:
66:```
67:/compact focus on Bundle ship cumulative state and active queue, drop verbose codex T1 verdict bodies
68:```
69:
70:Per `coordination.md §12` row 2: hint steered summary BEFORE rot dominates. Karpathy §5 Layer-3 compiled wiki is the equivalent persistent-storage discipline.
71:
72:## Supporting disciplines (Rank #4-#7)
73:
74:### Rank #4 — Rewind-first on failure (coordination.md §12)
75:
76:When an approach fails mid-iteration, prefer `/rewind` to last sound state over layering corrections. Drops failed-tail + retains good reads. Per Thariq table at `coordination.md §12`:
77:
78:| Situation | Use | Why |
79:|---|---|---|
80:| Explored N files, tried approach A, failed | **Rewind** to after-reads, re-prompt with learning | Keep expensive reads, drop failed attempt |
81:| Session bloated mid-task | `/compact <hint>` | Steered lossy summary |
82:| Genuinely new task | `/clear` + brief | Zero rot, controlled carry-over |
83:| High-volume exploration, only conclusion matters | Subagent fork | Tool noise GC'd on exit |
84:
85:### Rank #5 — Karpathy §5 3-layer progressive disclosure
86:
87:Per `karpathy-adapted.md §5 Wiki Compounding Surface`:
88:- **Layer 1 chronological log**: `.claude/state/*.jsonl` (codex_review_HEAD_*.jsonl / codex_consult_*_OUT.txt / mcp_health.jsonl / subagent_transcripts.jsonl)
89:- **Layer 2 index**: `.claude/projects/*/memory/MEMORY.md` (≤200 lines / 150-char one-line topic pointers)
90:- **Layer 3 compiled wiki**: `docs/karpathy-llm-wiki-practice.md` + `.claude/rules/*.md` + per-topic `memory/` files
91:
92:**MEMORY.md discipline**: NEVER write content directly; always one-line pointer with `[Title](file.md) — hook` format. If a top entry exceeds ~3000 chars, archive immediately (this rule's codification trigger).
93:
94:### Rank #6 — Pre-emptive arg truncation (deepagents pattern, doc-only port)
95:
96:Per `Z:/repos/deps/deepagents/.../summarization.py:122-149 @ 95f845d2`: TruncateArgsSettings TypedDict — for long-arc /loop fires touching Edit/Write/execute args with content >4KB, retain only first ~20-1000 chars of the arg with a `"[truncated — N chars; see file/commit for full content]"` placeholder. NOT active middleware in claude-sota-installed (no Python SDK runtime) — but discipline applies operationally: when args bloat, persist the full content to disk first (Write tool), then reference path in subsequent steps.
97:
98:### Rank #7 — Subagent forks for high-output exploration
99:
100:Per `parallel-agent-wave.md §Fork-vs-fresh routing` + `team-orch-patterns.md §1M context calibration`: dispatch tool-noisy exploration into subagent fork (forked = inherits parent context, conversation-history-cached) OR fresh subagent (zero parent context, isolation). The subagent's tool output GC'd on exit — only the final return enters parent context.
101:
102:## How to apply (operational checklist)
103:
104:For every long-arc /loop fire:
105:
106:1. **At session start**: check `[████░░░] N/1000k` context indicator. If >20% at start, immediately invoke `/compact <hint>` BEFORE any work
107:2. **For every >20-line probe**: route through `ctx_batch_execute` (multi-command + queries) OR `ctx_execute` (single command + intent)
108:3. **For every repo-scope audit ≥5 files**: pack with repomix BEFORE Reading individual files
109:4. **For every URL fetch**: prefer `ctx_fetch_and_index` over `WebFetch` (raw HTML stays in sandbox)
110:5. **At ~250k context**: invoke `/compact <hint>` pre-emptively (NOT wait for autocompact at 80%)
111:6. **At ~300k context**: STOP planning new work; ship current Bundle + commit + `/clear` between Bundle cycles
112:7. **On approach-failure**: rewind to last-sound-state with `/rewind`, NEVER layer corrections
113:8. **MEMORY.md hygiene**: if any entry exceeds ~300 chars or grows beyond a single line of pointer text, immediately archive + reset to proper one-line index format
114:
115:## Anti-patterns
116:
117:- **Let autocompact fire blind at 80%** — refuted by `karpathy-adapted.md §5` + Thariq tip 2026-04-16. Autocompact at the model's "least intelligent point" produces lossy summaries with no operator steering. Pre-emptive `/compact <hint>` is the SOTA.
118:- **Read large files into context for analysis** — refuted by file_writing_policy + ctx_execute discipline. Use `ctx_execute_file(path, code)` to process in sandbox; only printed answer enters context.
119:- **Bash for >20-line commands** — refuted by PreToolUse hook guidance. Route through `ctx_execute(language: shell)` or `ctx_batch_execute`.
120:- **Multiple ctx_execute calls when one batch suffices** — refuted by ctx_batch_execute description. One call replaces 30+ ctx_execute + 10+ ctx_search calls.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\claude-community\.claude-plugin\marketplace.json:11925:      "description": "# Munin for Claude Code\n\n> **Munin** is a long-term memory system for developers. Every time you work with Claude Code, Munin remembers your context, decisions, bugs, and solutions — so you never have to repeat yourself.\n\n**Munin** is your project's memory companion, integrated directly into Claude Code. It remembers your project, finds past errors, and suggests solutions based on what you've done before.\n\n---\n\n## Features\n\n### 🤖 Munin Agent\nCall `@munin` in any conversation to talk directly with your project's memory. Munin has full access to your memories — search, retrieve, store — all following the Memory Index Protocol.\n\n### 🔍 Automatic Memory Search\nEvery time you start a Claude Code session, Munin automatically finds relevant memories from your project. No need to ask — context is already there.\n\n### 💾 Session Summarization\nWhen a session ends, Munin automatically saves a summary to memory. Tasks, decisions, bugs — everything is recorded.\n\n### 📚 Error Catalog\nWhen an error occurs, Munin will:\n1. **Search** the error catalog first — a fix might already exist\n2. **Fix** following the precedent\n3. **Store** the new resolution so the next time is faster\n\n### 🧠 GraphRAG Memory\nEvery memory is automatically indexed into a knowledge graph:\n- **Entities** — services, libraries, models are extracted\n- **Relationships** — connections between components are mapped\n- **Semantic search** — find anything using natural language\n\n---\n\n## How It Works\n\n```\nYou open Claude Code\n        │\n        ▼\n  SessionStart Hook\n  → Munin searches relevant memories\n  → Context injected into conversation\n        │\n        ▼\n  You work on your task\n  → Munin monitors for errors\n  → Errors → Error Catalog\n        │\n        ▼\n  Session ends (or /compact)\n  → Summary saved to memory\n        │\n        ▼\n  Next session — Munin remembers\n```\n\n---\n\n## Quick Start\n\n### 1. Create a Free Munin Account\n\nSign up at [munin.kalera.app](https://munin.kalera.app) — FREE FOREVER.\n\nAfter signing up, create a new project and copy your **Project ID** (`proj_xxxxxxxxxxxx`).\n\n### 2. Add Your Project ID\n\nCreate or edit `.env` in your project root:\n\n```bash\nMUNIN_PROJECT=proj_your_project_id\n```\n\n### 3. Enable the MCP Server\n\n**Option A — Global (recommended, works in any project):**\n```bash\n# Download the Munin MCP wrapper\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/3d-era/munin/main/scripts/munin-mcp-wrapper.sh \\\n  -o ~/.claude/munin-mcp-wrapper.sh\nchmod +x ~/.claude/munin-mcp-wrapper.sh\n\n# Enable globally\nclaude mcp add --scope user munin bash ~/.claude/munin-mcp-wrapper.sh\n```\n\n**Option B — Project-specific:**\n```bash\nclaude mcp add --scope project munin bash -c '\n  ENV_FILE=\"$(pwd)/.env\"\n  if [[ -f \"$ENV_FILE\" ]]; then\n    while IFS=\"=\" read -r key rest; do\n      [[ \"$key\" =~ ^[[:space:]]*# ]] && continue\n      [[ -z \"$key\" ]] && continue\n      key=$(echo \"$key\" | sed \"s/^[[:space:]]*//;s/[[:space:]]*$//\")\n      value=$(echo \"$rest\" | sed \"s/^[[:space:]]*//;s/#.*$//;s/[[:space:]]*$//\")\n      [[ -n \"$value\" ]] && export \"$key=$value\" 2>/dev/null || true\n    done < \"$ENV_FILE\"\n  fi\n  exec npx @kalera/munin-claude mcp\n'\n```\n\n**Option C — From this repo (development):**\n```bash\nclaude mcp add --scope user munin bash -c '\n  PROJECT_DIR=\"${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$(pwd)}\"\n  ENV_FILE=\"$PROJECT_DIR/.env\"\n  if [[ -f \"$ENV_FILE\" ]]; then\n    grep -E \"^MUNIN_PROJECT=\" \"$ENV_FILE\" | while IFS=\"=\" read -r key val; do\n      export \"$key=$val\"\n    done\n  fi\n  exec npx @kalera/munin-claude mcp\n'\n```\n\n### 4. Start Using\n\n```bash\n# Talk to Munin directly\n@munion ask about the SePay integration\n@munion what did we decide about the auth system?\n\n# Or just start working — Munin auto-loads context\n# Munin will remember everything when you finish\n```\n\n---\n\n## Configuration\n\n### Environment Variables\n\n| Variable | Default | Description |\n|----------|---------|-------------|\n| `MUNIN_PROJECT` | *(required)* | Your project ID from munin.kalera.app |\n| `MUNIN_BASE_URL` | `https://munin.kalera.dev` | Munin API server |\n| `MUNIN_API_KEY` | *(from dashboard)* | API key for authentication |\n\n### Self-hosted Munin\n\nIf you're running your own Munin instance:\n\n```bash\nMUNIN_BASE_URL=https://your-munin-instance.com \\\nclaude mcp add --scope user munin bash -c \\\n  'MUNIN_BASE_URL=\"https://your-munin-instance.com\" exec npx @kalera/munin-claude mcp'\n```\n\n---\n\n## Skills — What Triggers Munin\n\nMunin activates automatically based on context:\n\n| Skill | Triggers | What It Does |\n|-------|----------|--------------|\n| `munin-memory` | \"search memory\", \"remember\", \"find in memory\" | Full memory operations |\n| `munin-architecture` | \"architecture\", \"tech stack\", \"system design\" | Architecture context |\n| `munin-error-catalog` | \"bug\", \"error\", \"crash\", \"doesn't work\" | Error resolution |\n\n---\n\n## Memory Protocol — How Munin Works\n\n### At Task Start\n1. `munin_search_memories` with keywords relevant to the task\n2. `munin_retrieve_memory` for detailed context\n3. Present findings — **verify from memory, don't guess**\n\n### At Task End\n`munin_store_memory` with:\n- **title**: Concise summary (max 80 chars)\n- **content**: Details including file paths, decisions, outcomes\n- **tags**: Categories (`task`, `architecture`, `bug-fix`, etc.)\n\n### On Bug Fixes\n1. Search error catalog first\n2. Present existing fix if found\n3. Fix and **store the new resolution**\n\n### On Decisions\nTag as `architecture` + `decision`, include timeline/version\n\n---\n\n## Pricing\n\n| Plan | Price | Memories | Projects |\n|------|-------|----------|----------|\n| **Free** | $0 | 500 | 3 |\n| Pro | $9/mo | Unlimited | Unlimited |\n| Team | $29/mo | Unlimited | Unlimited |\n\n**[Sign up at munin.kalera.app →](https://munin.kalera.app)**\n\n---\n\n## Resources\n\n| Resource | Link                                                                                           |\n|----------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| 🌐 **Website** | [munin.kalera.app](https://munin.kalera.app)                                                   |\n| 📖 **Docs** | [munin.kalera.app/docs](https://munin.kalera.app/docs)                                         |\n| 💬 **Discord** | Coming soon                                                                                    |\n| 🐛 **Issues** | [github.com/3d-era/munin-for-agents/issues](https://github.com/3d-era/munin-for-agents/issues) |\n| 📦 **npm** | [npmjs.com/package/@kalera/munin-claude](https://npmjs.com/package/@kalera/munin-claude)       |\n| 🔌 **Gemini Extension** | [github.com/3d-era/munin-gemini-extension](https://github.com/3d-era/munin-gemini-extension)   |\n\n---\n\n## License\n\nMIT — [github.com/3d-era/munin-for-agents](https://github.com/3d-era/munin-for-agents)",

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\docs\platform-support.md:175:When OpenCode triggers `experimental.session.compacting` (auto on context overflow OR manual `/compact`), context-mode saves a snapshot to its per-project SQLite store. The NEXT new session in the same project — typically after `Ctrl+D` then re-running `opencode`, or starting a fresh chat — claims that snapshot via `experimental.chat.system.transform` and prepends it to `system[1]` (preserves OpenCode's `[header, body]` cache fold). The current session never claims its OWN snapshot back (self-injection guard, v1.0.106). To verify the injection landed, run with `OPENCODE_DEBUG=1` and grep for `<!-- context-mode v` in the system prompt — that's the visible marker.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:34:Every MCP tool call dumps raw data into your context window. A Playwright snapshot costs 56 KB. Twenty GitHub issues cost 59 KB. One access log — 45 KB. After 30 minutes, 40% of your context is gone. And when the agent compacts the conversation to free space, it forgets which files it was editing, what tasks are in progress, and what you last asked for. On top of that, the agent wastes output tokens on filler, pleasantries, and verbose explanations — burning context from both sides.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:41:2. **Session Continuity** — Every file edit, git operation, task, error, and user decision is tracked in SQLite. When the conversation compacts, context-mode doesn't dump this data back into context — it indexes events into FTS5 and retrieves only what's relevant via BM25 search. The model picks up exactly where you left off. If you don't `--continue`, previous session data is deleted immediately — a fresh session means a clean slate.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:86:**Routing:** Automatic. The SessionStart hook injects routing instructions at runtime — no file is written to your project. The plugin registers all hooks (PreToolUse, PostToolUse, PreCompact, SessionStart) and 11 MCP tools — six sandbox tools (`ctx_batch_execute`, `ctx_execute`, `ctx_execute_file`, `ctx_index`, `ctx_search`, `ctx_fetch_and_index`) plus five meta-tools (`ctx_stats`, `ctx_doctor`, `ctx_upgrade`, `ctx_purge`, `ctx_insight`).

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:939:> **Note:** Full session continuity (capture + snapshot + restore) works on **Claude Code**, **Gemini CLI**, **VS Code Copilot**, **JetBrains Copilot**, **OpenCode**, and **KiloCode**. **OpenCode** and **KiloCode** use `experimental.chat.system.transform` as a SessionStart surrogate to inject the routing block and restore prior sessions, plus `chat.message` for user-prompt capture; full SessionStart hook support is not yet available ([#14808](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/14808), [#5409](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/5409)), but prior-session continuity and user-decision capture work fully. **Cursor** captures tool events via `preToolUse`/`postToolUse`, but `sessionStart` is currently rejected by Cursor's validator ([forum report](https://forum.cursor.com/t/unknown-hook-type-sessionstart/149566)), so session restore after compaction is not available yet. **OpenClaw** uses native gateway plugin hooks (`api.on()`) for full session continuity. **Pi Coding Agent** provides high session continuity via extension hooks (`tool_call`, `tool_result`, `session_start`, `session_before_compact`). **Codex CLI** provides partial hook-based session tracking through PreToolUse, PostToolUse, SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, and Stop; MCP tools work. **Antigravity**, **Kiro**, and **Zed** have no hook support in the current release, so session tracking is not available.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1022:**Claude Code** — Full session support. All 5 hook types fire, capturing tool events, user decisions, building compaction snapshots, and restoring state after compaction, `--continue`, `--resume`, or `/resume`.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1024:**Gemini CLI** — High coverage. PostToolUse (AfterTool), PreCompact (PreCompress), and SessionStart all fire. Missing UserPromptSubmit, so user decisions and corrections aren't captured — but file edits, git ops, errors, and tasks are fully tracked.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1026:**VS Code Copilot** — High coverage. Same as Gemini CLI — PostToolUse, PreCompact, and SessionStart all fire. User decisions aren't captured but all tool-level events are.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1028:**JetBrains Copilot** — High coverage. Same capabilities as VS Code Copilot — PostToolUse, PreCompact, and SessionStart all fire. Uses the same hook wire protocol and response format. User decisions aren't captured but all tool-level events are.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1032:**OpenCode** — Full session support. The TypeScript plugin captures PostToolUse events via `tool.execute.after`, user prompts and decisions via `chat.message`, builds compaction snapshots via `experimental.session.compacting`, and restores prior sessions via `experimental.chat.system.transform` (SessionStart surrogate). Routing block is injected on first `chat.system.transform` per session. AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md/CONTEXT.md rules are captured automatically on first hook fire.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1034:**KiloCode** — Full session support. Shares the same plugin architecture as OpenCode via the OpenCodeAdapter. The TypeScript plugin captures PostToolUse events via `tool.execute.after`, user prompts and decisions via `chat.message`, builds compaction snapshots via `experimental.session.compacting`, and restores prior sessions via `experimental.chat.system.transform` (SessionStart surrogate).
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1036:**OpenClaw / Pi Agent** — High coverage. All tool lifecycle hooks (`after_tool_call`, `before_compaction`, `session_start`) fire via the native gateway plugin. User decisions aren't captured but file edits, git ops, errors, and tasks are fully tracked. Falls back to DB snapshot reconstruction if compaction hooks fail on older gateway versions. See [`docs/adapters/openclaw.md`](docs/adapters/openclaw.md).
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1044:**Kiro** — Partial coverage. Native `preToolUse` and `postToolUse` hooks capture tool events and enforce sandbox routing. `agentSpawn` (the Kiro equivalent of SessionStart) is not yet implemented, so session restore after compaction is not available. Requires manually copying `KIRO.md` to your project root. Auto-detected via MCP protocol handshake (`clientInfo.name`).
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:1046:**Pi Coding Agent** — High coverage. The extension registers all key lifecycle events: `tool_call` (PreToolUse), `tool_result` (PostToolUse), `session_start` (SessionStart), and `session_before_compact` (PreCompact). File edits, git ops, errors, and tasks are fully tracked. Session restore after compaction works via the extension's event hooks.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\docs\adapters\openclaw.md:100:If compaction hooks fail to fire (e.g., on older OpenClaw versions), the adapter falls back to **DB snapshot reconstruction** — rebuilding session state from the events already persisted in SQLite by `after_tool_call`. .claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\docs\adapters\openclaw.md:115:**What breaks on older versions:** Lifecycle hooks registered via `api.on()` — including `before_compaction`, `after_compaction`, `session_start`, and tool interception hooks — may silently fail to fire.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\docs\adapters\openclaw.md:117:**Graceful degradation:** If compaction hooks don't fire, the adapter falls back to DB snapshot reconstruction, rebuilding session state from events already persisted by `after_tool_call`. This produces a less precise snapshot than the PreCompact path but preserves critical state (active files, tasks, errors). The adapter will not crash on older versions, but compaction recovery quality will be reduced.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\anthropic-agent-skills\skills\claude-api\SKILL.md:203:**Critical:** Append `response.content` (not just the text) back to your messages on every turn. Compaction blocks in the response must be preserved — the API uses them to replace the compacted history on the next request. Extracting only the text string and appending that will silently lose the compaction state.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\anthropic-agent-skills\skills\claude-api\shared\tool-use-concepts.md:247:Context editing clears stale tool results and thinking blocks from the transcript as a long-running agent accumulates turns. Unlike compaction (which summarizes), context editing prunes — the cleared content is removed, not replaced. Use it when old tool outputs are no longer relevant and you want to keep the transcript lean without losing the conversation structure. Thresholds for what to clear are configurable.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\anthropic-agent-skills\skills\claude-api\shared\prompt-caching.md:79:**Fork operations must reuse the parent's exact prefix.** Side computations (summarization, compaction, sub-agents) often spin up a separate API call. .claude\plugins\marketplaces\anthropic-agent-skills\skills\claude-api\shared\model-migration.md:598:Claude Opus 4.7 and Claude Opus 4.6 count tokens differently. The same input text produces a higher token count on Claude Opus 4.7 than on Claude Opus 4.6, and `/v1/messages/count_tokens` will return a different number of tokens for Claude Opus 4.7 than it did for Claude Opus 4.6. The token efficiency of Claude Opus 4.7 can vary by workload shape. Prompting interventions, `task_budget`, and `effort` can help control costs and ensure appropriate token usage. Keep in mind that these controls may trade off model intelligence. **Update your `max_tokens` parameters to give additional headroom, including compaction triggers.** Claude Opus 4.7 provides a 1M context window at standard API pricing with no long-context premium.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\everything-claude-code\docs\TROUBLESHOOTING.md:39:### MCP connectors look connected but fail after compaction
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\everything-claude-code\docs\TROUBLESHOOTING.md:41:**Symptoms:** Gmail or Google Drive MCP tools fail after compaction even though the connector still looks authenticated in the UI.

1:#!/usr/bin/env node
2:/**
3: * PreCompact Hook - Save state before context compaction
4: *
5: * Cross-platform (Windows, macOS, Linux)
6: *
7: * Runs before Claude compacts context, giving you a chance to
8: * preserve important state that might get lost in summarization.
9: */
10:
11:const path = require('path');
12:const {
13:  getSessionsDir,
14:  getDateTimeString,
15:  getTimeString,
16:  findFiles,
17:  ensureDir,
18:  appendFile,
19:  log
20:} = require('../lib/utils');
21:
22:async function main() {
23:  const sessionsDir = getSessionsDir();
24:  const compactionLog = path.join(sessionsDir, 'compaction-log.txt');
25:
26:  ensureDir(sessionsDir);
27:
28:  // Log compaction event with timestamp
29:  const timestamp = getDateTimeString();
30:  appendFile(compactionLog, `[${timestamp}] Context compaction triggered\n`);
31:
32:  // If there's an active session file, note the compaction
33:  const sessions = findFiles(sessionsDir, '*-session.tmp');
34:
35:  if (sessions.length > 0) {
36:    const activeSession = sessions[0].path;
37:    const timeStr = getTimeString();
38:    appendFile(activeSession, `\n---\n**[Compaction occurred at ${timeStr}]** - Context was summarized\n`);
39:  }
40:
41:  log('[PreCompact] State saved before compaction');
42:  process.exit(0);
43:}
44:
45:main().catch(err => {
46:  console.error('[PreCompact] Error:', err.message);
47:  process.exit(0);
48:});

1:---
2:name: strategic-compact
3:description: Suggests manual context compaction at logical intervals to preserve context through task phases rather than arbitrary auto-compaction.
4:origin: ECC
5:---
6:
7:# Strategic Compact Skill
8:
9:Suggests manual `/compact` at strategic points in your workflow rather than relying on arbitrary auto-compaction.
10:
11:## When to Activate
12:
13:- Running long sessions that approach context limits (200K+ tokens)
14:- Working on multi-phase tasks (research → plan → implement → test)
15:- Switching between unrelated tasks within the same session
16:- After completing a major milestone and starting new work
17:- When responses slow down or become less coherent (context pressure)
18:
19:## Why Strategic Compaction?
20:
21:Auto-compaction triggers at arbitrary points:
22:- Often mid-task, losing important context
23:- No awareness of logical task boundaries
24:- Can interrupt complex multi-step operations
25:
26:Strategic compaction at logical boundaries:
27:- **After exploration, before execution** — Compact research context, keep implementation plan
28:- **After completing a milestone** — Fresh start for next phase
29:- **Before major context shifts** — Clear exploration context before different task
30:
31:## How It Works
32:
33:The `suggest-compact.js` script runs on PreToolUse (Edit/Write) and:
34:
35:1. **Tracks tool calls** — Counts tool invocations in session
36:2. **Threshold detection** — Suggests at configurable threshold (default: 50 calls)
37:3. **Periodic reminders** — Reminds every 25 calls after threshold
38:
39:## Hook Setup
40:
41:Add to your `~/.claude/settings.json`:
42:
43:```json
44:{
45:  "hooks": {
46:    "PreToolUse": [
47:      {
48:        "matcher": "Edit",
49:        "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "node ~/.claude/skills/strategic-compact/suggest-compact.js" }]
50:      },
51:      {
52:        "matcher": "Write",
53:        "hooks": [{ "type": "command", "command": "node ~/.claude/skills/strategic-compact/suggest-compact.js" }]
54:      }
55:    ]
56:  }
57:}
58:```
59:
60:## Configuration
61:
62:Environment variables:
63:- `COMPACT_THRESHOLD` — Tool calls before first suggestion (default: 50)
64:
65:## Compaction Decision Guide
66:
67:Use this table to decide when to compact:
68:
69:| Phase Transition | Compact? | Why |
70:|-----------------|----------|-----|
71:| Research → Planning | Yes | Research context is bulky; plan is the distilled output |
72:| Planning → Implementation | Yes | Plan is in TodoWrite or a file; free up context for code |
73:| Implementation → Testing | Maybe | Keep if tests reference recent code; compact if switching focus |
74:| Debugging → Next feature | Yes | Debug traces pollute context for unrelated work |
75:| Mid-implementation | No | Losing variable names, file paths, and partial state is costly |
76:| After a failed approach | Yes | Clear the dead-end reasoning before trying a new approach |
77:
78:## What Survives Compaction
79:
80:Understanding what persists helps you compact with confidence:
81:
82:| Persists | Lost |
83:|----------|------|
84:| CLAUDE.md instructions | Intermediate reasoning and analysis |
85:| TodoWrite task list | File contents you previously read |
86:| Memory files (`~/.claude/memory/`) | Multi-step conversation context |
87:| Git state (commits, branches) | Tool call history and counts |
88:| Files on disk | Nuanced user preferences stated verbally |
89:
90:## Best Practices
91:
92:1. **Compact after planning** — Once plan is finalized in TodoWrite, compact to start fresh
93:2. **Compact after debugging** — Clear error-resolution context before continuing
94:3. **Don't compact mid-implementation** — Preserve context for related changes
95:4. **Read the suggestion** — The hook tells you *when*, you decide *if*
96:5. **Write before compacting** — Save important context to files or memory before compacting
97:6. **Use `/compact` with a summary** — Add a custom message: `/compact Focus on implementing auth middleware next`
98:
99:## Token Optimization Patterns
100:
101:### Trigger-Table Lazy Loading
102:Instead of loading full skill content at session start, use a trigger table that maps keywords to skill paths. Skills load only when triggered, reducing baseline context by 50%+:
103:
104:| Trigger | Skill | Load When |
105:|---------|-------|-----------|
106:| "test", "tdd", "coverage" | tdd-workflow | User mentions testing |
107:| "security", "auth", "xss" | security-review | Security-related work |
108:| "deploy", "ci/cd" | deployment-patterns | Deployment context |
109:
110:### Context Composition Awareness
111:Monitor what's consuming your context window:
112:- **CLAUDE.md files** — Always loaded, keep lean
113:- **Loaded skills** — Each skill adds 1-5K tokens
114:- **Conversation history** — Grows with each exchange
115:- **Tool results** — File reads, search results add bulk
116:
117:### Duplicate Instruction Detection
118:Common sources of duplicate context:
119:- Same rules in both `~/.claude/rules/` and project `.claude/rules/`
120:- Skills that repeat CLAUDE.md instructions

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:34:Every MCP tool call dumps raw data into your context window. A Playwright snapshot costs 56 KB. Twenty GitHub issues cost 59 KB. One access log — 45 KB. After 30 minutes, 40% of your context is gone. And when the agent compacts the conversation to free space, it forgets which files it was editing, what tasks are in progress, and what you last asked for. On top of that, the agent wastes output tokens on filler, pleasantries, and verbose explanations — burning context from both sides.
.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:41:2. **Session Continuity** — Every file edit, git operation, task, error, and user decision is tracked in SQLite. When the conversation compacts, context-mode doesn't dump this data back into context — it indexes events into FTS5 and retrieves only what's relevant via BM25 search. The model picks up exactly where you left off. If you don't `--continue`, previous session data is deleted immediately — a fresh session means a clean slate.

.claude\plugins\marketplaces\context-mode\README.md:939:> **Note:** Full session continuity (capture + snapshot + restore) works on **Claude Code**, **Gemini CLI**, **VS Code Copilot**, **JetBrains Copilot**, **OpenCode**, and **KiloCode**. **OpenCode** and **KiloCode** use `experimental.chat.system.transform` as a SessionStart surrogate to inject the routing block and restore prior sessions, plus `chat.message` for user-prompt capture; full SessionStart hook support is not yet available ([#14808](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/14808), [#5409](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/5409)), but prior-session continuity and user-decision capture work fully. **Cursor** captures tool events via `preToolUse`/`postToolUse`, but `sessionStart` is currently rejected by Cursor's validator ([forum report](https://forum.cursor.com/t/unknown-hook-type-sessionstart/149566)), so session restore after compaction is not available yet. **OpenClaw** uses native gateway plugin hooks (`api.on()`) for full session continuity. **Pi Coding Agent** provides high session continuity via extension hooks (`tool_call`, `tool_result`, `session_start`, `session_before_compact`). **Codex CLI** provides partial hook-based session tracking through PreToolUse, PostToolUse, SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, and Stop; MCP tools work. **Antigravity**, **Kiro**, and **Zed** have no hook support in the current release, so session tracking is not available.

1:#!/usr/bin/env node
2:/**
3: * PreCompact hook for context-mode session continuity.
4: *
5: * Triggered when Claude Code is about to compact the conversation.
6: * Reads all captured session events, builds a priority-sorted resume
7: * snapshot (<2KB XML), and stores it for injection after compact.
8: *
9: * Crash-resilience: wrapped via runHook (#414).
10: */
11:
12:import { runHook } from "./run-hook.mjs";
13:
14:await runHook(async () => {
15:  const {
16:    readStdin,
17:    parseStdin,
18:    getSessionId,
19:    getSessionDBPath,
20:    resolveConfigDir,
21:  } = await import("./session-helpers.mjs");
22:  const { createSessionLoaders } = await import("./session-loaders.mjs");
23:  const { appendFileSync } = await import("node:fs");
24:  const { join, dirname } = await import("node:path");
25:  const { fileURLToPath } = await import("node:url");
26:
27:  // Resolve absolute path for imports
28:  const HOOK_DIR = dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
29:  const { loadSessionDB, loadSnapshot } = createSessionLoaders(HOOK_DIR);
30:  const DEBUG_LOG = join(resolveConfigDir(), "context-mode", "precompact-debug.log");
31:
32:  try {
33:    const raw = await readStdin();
34:    const input = parseStdin(raw);
35:
36:    const { buildResumeSnapshot } = await loadSnapshot();
37:    const { SessionDB } = await loadSessionDB();
38:
39:    const dbPath = getSessionDBPath();
40:    const db = new SessionDB({ dbPath });
41:    const sessionId = getSessionId(input);
42:
43:    // Get all events for this session
44:    const events = db.getEvents(sessionId);
45:
46:    if (events.length > 0) {
47:      const stats = db.getSessionStats(sessionId);
48:      const snapshot = buildResumeSnapshot(events, {
49:        compactCount: (stats?.compact_count ?? 0) + 1,
50:      });
51:
52:      db.upsertResume(sessionId, snapshot, events.length);
53:      db.incrementCompactCount(sessionId);
54:
55:      // Write compaction category event for analytics
56:      const fileEvents = events.filter(e => e.category === "file");
57:      db.insertEvent(sessionId, {
58:        type: "compaction_summary",
59:        category: "compaction",
60:        data: `Session compacted. ${events.length} events, ${fileEvents.length} files touched.`,
61:        priority: 1,
62:      }, "PreCompact");
63:    }
64:
65:    db.close();
66:  } catch (err) {
67:    try {
68:      appendFileSync(DEBUG_LOG, `[${new Date().toISOString()}] ${err.message}\n`);
69:    } catch {
70:      // Silent fallback
71:    }
72:  }
73:
74:  // PreCompact doesn't need hookSpecificOutput
75:  console.log(JSON.stringify({}));
76:});

939:> **Note:** Full session continuity (capture + snapshot + restore) works on **Claude Code**, **Gemini CLI**, **VS Code Copilot**, **JetBrains Copilot**, **OpenCode**, and **KiloCode**. **OpenCode** and **KiloCode** use `experimental.chat.system.transform` as a SessionStart surrogate to inject the routing block and restore prior sessions, plus `chat.message` for user-prompt capture; full SessionStart hook support is not yet available ([#14808](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/14808), [#5409](https://github.com/sst/opencode/issues/5409)), but prior-session continuity and user-decision capture work fully. **Cursor** captures tool events via `preToolUse`/`postToolUse`, but `sessionStart` is currently rejected by Cursor's validator ([forum report](https://forum.cursor.com/t/unknown-hook-type-sessionstart/149566)), so session restore after compaction is not available yet. **OpenClaw** uses native gateway plugin hooks (`api.on()`) for full session continuity. **Pi Coding Agent** provides high session continuity via extension hooks (`tool_call`, `tool_result`, `session_start`, `session_before_compact`). **Codex CLI** provides partial hook-based session tracking through PreToolUse, PostToolUse, SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, and Stop; MCP tools work. **Antigravity**, **Kiro**, and **Zed** have no hook support in the current release, so session tracking is not available.

944:Every tool call passes through hooks that extract structured events:
945:
946:| Category | Events | Priority | Captured By |
947:|---|---|---|---|
948:| **Files** | read, edit, write, glob, grep | Critical (P1) | PostToolUse |

950:| **Plans** | enter, exit, approved, rejected, file write | Critical (P1) | PostToolUse |
951:| **Rules** | CLAUDE.md / GEMINI.md / AGENTS.md paths + content | Critical (P1) | SessionStart |
952:| **User Prompts** | Every user message (for last-prompt restore) | Critical (P1) | UserPromptSubmit |
953:| **Decisions** | User corrections, preferences ("use X instead", "don't do Y") | High (P2) | UserPromptSubmit |
954:| **Git** | checkout, commit, merge, rebase, stash, push, pull, diff, status | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
955:| **Errors** | Tool failures, non-zero exit codes | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
956:| **Error Resolution** | Error → fix pairs detected across sequential tool calls | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
957:| **Constraints** | Discovered limitations ("not supported", "permission denied") | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
958:| **Blockers** | "blocked on", "waiting for", "depends on" — tracked until resolved | High (P2) | UserPromptSubmit |
959:| **Rejected Approaches** | Tool calls denied by user (PreToolUse → PostToolUse marker) | High (P2) | PreToolUse |
960:| **Environment** | cwd changes, venv, nvm, conda, worktree, package installs | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
961:| **Agent Findings** | Completed subagent results (first 500 chars) | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
962:| **Iteration Loops** | Same tool called 3+ times with similar input (retry detection) | High (P2) | PostToolUse |
963:| **Latency** | Tool calls exceeding 5s (tool name + duration in ms) | Normal (P3) | PreToolUse |
964:| **MCP Tools** | All `mcp__*` tool calls with usage counts | Normal (P3) | PostToolUse |
965:| **Subagents** | Agent tool launches and completions | Normal (P3) | PostToolUse |
966:| **Skills** | Slash command invocations | Normal (P3) | PostToolUse |
967:| **External Refs** | URLs, GitHub issue references (#123), deduped | Normal (P3) | PostToolUse |
968:| **Role** | Persona / behavioral directives ("act as senior engineer") | Normal (P3) | UserPromptSubmit |
969:| **Intent** | Session mode classification (investigate, implement, review) | Low (P4) | UserPromptSubmit |
970:| **Data** | Large user-pasted data references (>1 KB) | Low (P4) | UserPromptSubmit |

17:A repo qualifies as **SOTA** for eee runtime adoption if and only if it satisfies **multi-dimensional convergence** across 10 independent dimensions, with **use-class precision** per dimension. Flat criteria ("AGPL = REJECT" / "stars >500") produce false-positive rejects + miss real risk. Convergence gate = ≥7 of 10 dimensions PASS for use-class, with no critical dimension (D1 license-use-class blocking, D6 use-class incompatible) FAILED.
19:**Replacement viability**: any proposed replacement MUST satisfy D1-D10 AND be ≤ incumbent's last-push age (D2 freshness gate). A staler replacement is NOT a valid SOTA recommendation.
29:5. License-context question (any non-MIT / non-Apache / non-BSD license)
35:### D1 — License-use-class precision

39:| License | CLI-binary-use | Library-link | Network-served | SaaS-distributed |

1:---
2:name: sota-research-architecture
3:description: Multi-dimensional convergence gate for defining SOTA. Supersedes flat audit-criteria with use-class-precise license analysis + replacement-freshness gate + 10-dimension convergence lattice. Cardinal-rule-1 cite-trail authority for SOTA verdicts in eee runtime.

6:
7:# SOTA Research Architecture (SRA) — multi-dimensional convergence gate
8:
9:# Reference: TIER-1-DIRECT user directive 2026-05-08 verbatim "improve your research architecture, you need to make sure they have the ability to gather muti dimensional informaiton, github and beyond, to find the convergence, it is the very foundation of our entire architecutre" — codifies the methodology improvement mandate triggered by Wave 102 audit's over-applied REJECT verdicts (Ship 2T trufflehog AGPL-3.0 over-applied to CLI-binary-use; KuzuDB recommended at 7-month-stale despite "SOTA" framing).
10:
11:# Reference: TIER-2 sister `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/convergence-gate.md` — Axis-1+2+3 convergence-gate (org-distinct + named-T2 + ≥3-month stable). SRA EXTENDS that 3-axis gate to 10-dimension convergence with use-class precision.
12:
13:# Reference: TIER-2 sister `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md` Probe 6 license-blocker — SRA D1 dimension is the use-class-precise refinement of Probe 6 (Probe 6 is blanket REJECT on AGPL/GPL/SSPL; SRA D1 distinguishes CLI-binary-use vs library-link vs network-served vs SaaS-distributed).
14:
15:## The rule
16:
17:A repo qualifies as **SOTA** for eee runtime adoption if and only if it satisfies **multi-dimensional convergence** across 10 independent dimensions, with **use-class precision** per dimension. Flat criteria ("AGPL = REJECT" / "stars >500") produce false-positive rejects + miss real risk. Convergence gate = ≥7 of 10 dimensions PASS for use-class, with no critical dimension (D1 license-use-class blocking, D6 use-class incompatible) FAILED.
18:
19:**Replacement viability**: any proposed replacement MUST satisfy D1-D10 AND be ≤ incumbent's last-push age (D2 freshness gate). A staler replacement is NOT a valid SOTA recommendation.
20:
21:## When this rule applies
22:
23:ANY of these triggers fires SRA:
24:
25:1. Architecture audit identifying REJECT/DOWNGRADE candidate
26:2. New repo/dep adoption decision
27:3. Replacement of existing dep with alternative
28:4. Plugin install candidate evaluation
29:5. License-context question (any non-MIT / non-Apache / non-BSD license)
30:6. SOTA-freshness verification of any cite anchor
31:7. Named-author endorsement claim verification
32:
33:## The 10 dimensions
34:
35:### D1 — License-use-class precision
36:
37:Distinguish use-class BEFORE applying license clauses. License clause activation per sub-class:
38:
39:| License | CLI-binary-use | Library-link | Network-served | SaaS-distributed |
40:|---|---|---|---|---|
41:| MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
42:| LGPL-2.1/3.0 | ✅ | dynamic-link OK; static-link infects | ✅ | ✅ |
43:| GPL-2.0/3.0 | ✅ (no source-distribute mandate) | infects derivative | requires source-disclosure | full GPL-class restrictions |
44:| AGPL-3.0 | ✅ (local-CLI use) | infects derivative + network-trigger | requires source-disclosure | full AGPL-class restrictions |
45:| SSPL-1.0 | ✅ (local DB) | ✅ | ✅ | restricts SaaS-as-product |
46:| Elastic License 2.0 (ELv2) | ✅ (local plugin) | ✅ | ✅ | restricts managed-service-resale |
47:| Proprietary (commercial) | per-license-terms | per-license-terms | per-license-terms | per-license-terms |
48:| **NO LICENSE FILE** | undefined; default copyright; technically no permission to use | undefined | undefined | undefined |
49:
50:**Probe 6 application** is use-class-aware:
51:- AGPL CLI binary used to scan local files = ✅ ACCEPTABLE for eee
52:- AGPL library linked into eee runtime code = ❌ REJECT (license infects derivative)
53:- AGPL network-served as part of eee = ❌ REJECT (requires source-disclosure)
54:- ELv2 plugin loaded into eee runtime = ✅ ACCEPTABLE (eee is not SaaS-resale)
55:- SSPL DB used as local backend = ✅ ACCEPTABLE (eee is not DB-as-a-service product)
56:
57:**eee use-class baseline**: local autonomous /loop runtime; NOT distributed-as-product; NOT network-hosted-for-third-parties; NOT SaaS-resale. This use-class makes most non-permissive licenses ACCEPTABLE for inclusion (with disclosure).
58:
59:### D2 — SOTA-freshness gate (replacement-must-be-more-current)
60:
61:For any proposed REPLACEMENT:
62:- Compute `incumbent_last_push_age` = (now - incumbent's pushed_at)
63:- Compute `replacement_last_push_age` = (now - replacement's pushed_at)
64:- **MUST**: replacement_last_push_age ≤ incumbent_last_push_age
65:- IF replacement is staler than incumbent → REJECT REPLACEMENT, keep incumbent
66:
67:For incumbents:
68:- last-push < 30 days = ACTIVE
69:- 30-90 days = MAINTAINED
70:- 90-180 days = STABLE-BURN-IN (acceptable for mature deps)
71:- 180-365 days = STALE (DOWNGRADE flag)
72:- > 365 days = DORMANT (REJECT unless explicit ABANDONED-BUT-WORKING justification)
73:
74:### D3 — Star-velocity vs content-depth (fresh-paint detection)
75:
76:Calculate signals:
77:- `velocity = stars / age-days`
78:- `density = repo-size-KB / max(stars, 1)`
79:
80:Anti-pattern signals:
81:- `velocity > 100 stars/day` AND `density < 0.5 KB/star` → FRESH-PAINT SUSPECT
82:- Squashed git history + age <30 days + 1K+ stars → FRESH-PAINT (per `convergence-gate.md` §Anti-pattern)
83:- High star count BUT no named-practitioner endorsement → DOWNGRADE
84:
85:Verify: Read README. Confirm content matches star count. Inspect git history for organic vs squashed-import pattern.
86:
87:### D4 — Maintainer provenance tier
88:
89:| Tier | Definition | Convergence weight |
90:|---|---|---|
91:| **TIER-1-OFFICIAL** | Anthropic / OpenAI / Microsoft / Google / Apache / Linux Foundation / etc | STRONG-PROVENANCE-EXPRESS |
92:| **TIER-2-NAMED-PRACTITIONER** | Karpathy / Pocock / Osmani / Cherny / named-author with public track record | strong |
93:| **TIER-3-NAMED-ORG** | small org with named maintainers + multi-contributor | acceptable |
94:| **TIER-4-NAMED-INDIVIDUAL** | single named individual + active commit history | acceptable-with-disclosure |
95:| **TIER-5-UNKNOWN** | anonymous OR single-org with no named practitioners | DOWNGRADE; verify before adoption |
96:
97:**NO LICENSE FILE on TIER-1 or TIER-2 repo**: not a REJECT — verify the named-author intent (e.g., GitHub README declaration "MIT" without LICENSE file is COMMON for prototypes; cite-import is acceptable with disclosure).
98:
99:### D5 — Active maintenance signals (beyond last-push)
100:
101:- Issue-close-rate (closed / opened over rolling 90-day window) > 30% = active
102:- PR-merge-rate (merged / opened) > 40% = active
103:- Contributor diversity (number of distinct contributors past 90 days) > 1 = healthy
104:- Recent release cadence (releases per 90 days) > 0 = shipping
105:
106:If 0 of 4 signals = ABANDONED; if 1-2 = STALE-MAYBE-MAINTAINED (probe further); if 3-4 = ACTIVE.
107:
108:### D6 — Use-class compatibility (mode-harness-shape Probe 5)
109:
110:Does the repo's INTENDED use-mode match eee runtime?
111:
112:| Repo mode | eee compatibility |
113:|---|---|
114:| Autonomous /loop runtime | ✅ |
115:| Human-in-loop interactive | ✅ (subagent or operator-direct) |
116:| HARD-GATE pause-resume only | ❌ (incompatible with autonomous /loop; e.g., superpowers brainstorming) |
117:| Meta-skill TDD-for-skills harness | ❌ if claude-sota lacks TDD-for-skills test framework (e.g., superpowers writing-skills) |
118:| TIER-1 OFFICIAL CC plugin format | ✅ (Anthropic-curated for CC runtime) |
119:| Standalone CLI tool | ✅ (binary use-class) |
120:| Library/SDK requiring code-link | ✅ if license-link compatible (D1) |
121:
122:### D7 — Anthropic CC official policy alignment
123:
124:Probe Anthropic's actual policy:
125:- `code.claude.com/docs/en/*` — explicit license policy statements
126:- `anthropics/claude-plugins-official` marketplace — does Anthropic ship plugins under similar license? (If Anthropic ships X plugin under SSPL, SSPL is OK by Anthropic standards.)
127:- Anthropic CHANGELOG — license-acceptability statements
128:- `cookbook.anthropic.com` — examples ship under what licenses
129:
130:If Anthropic OFFICIALLY ships under license X for similar use-case → license X is acceptable. Don't apply stricter standard than Anthropic itself.
131:
132:### D8 — Industry adoption signal
133:
134:- Multi-org adoption: ≥3 distinct orgs use this repo in production
135:- Named-practitioner endorsements with dated artifact (tweet / blog / talk) ≥ 2
136:- Conference talks / academic papers cite it
137:- Posted to mailing lists / official Anthropic Discord / GitHub Discussions / forums
138:
139:A repo with high stars but ZERO industry adoption signals = popularity without depth.
140:
141:### D9 — Failure-mode awareness
142:
143:Per `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/named-failure-modes.md` catalog:
144:- Known FM-class triggered by this repo
145:- Recovery path documented OR pattern-match against existing FM
146:- CVE / security advisories
147:- Issue tracker patterns
148:
149:A repo with documented FM-class still ACCEPTABLE if recovery path is clear; UNDOCUMENTED-FAIL-CLASS = DOWNGRADE.
150:
151:### D10 — Replacement viability (when proposing alternatives)
152:
153:If proposing X → Y replacement:
154:- Y must satisfy D1-D9 INDEPENDENTLY for the use-class
155:- Y must satisfy D2 freshness gate (Y_last_push_age ≤ X_last_push_age)
156:- Y must FUNCTIONALLY cover same surface as X (not just claim coverage)
157:- Y must have D7 Anthropic-aligned position OR named-T1 cite
158:
159:**A replacement that is staler than the incumbent is NOT a valid recommendation.** Period.
160:
161:## Convergence verdict (decision lattice)
162:
163:Score each repo across D1-D10:
164:- D1 PASS for use-class = +1 (CRITICAL — D1 FAIL = blocking)
165:- D2 freshness PASS = +1
166:- D3 fresh-paint clear = +1
167:- D4 maintainer-provenance ≥ TIER-3 = +1
168:- D5 active-maintenance signals ≥ 2/4 = +1
169:- D6 use-class compatible = +1 (CRITICAL — D6 FAIL = blocking)
170:- D7 Anthropic-aligned OR not-required = +1
171:- D8 industry adoption ≥ 1 = +1
172:- D9 FM-class clear OR documented-recovery = +1
173:- D10 replacement gate (if applicable) PASS = +1
174:
175:**Verdict thresholds**:
176:
177:| Score | Verdict | Action |
178:|---|---|---|
179:| 9-10 + critical D1+D6 PASS | **INSTALL** | adopt; full SOTA confirmed |
180:| 7-8 + critical D1+D6 PASS | **DOWNGRADE-WITH-DISCLOSURE** | adopt with documentation of caveats |
181:| 5-6 OR critical D1/D6 ambiguous | **DEFER** | needs more probes (HONEST-NON-FINDING) |
182:| <5 OR critical D1+D6 FAIL | **REJECT** | does not satisfy SRA |
183:
184:## Cross-model T1 verification mandate
185:
186:Per CR-3 + Ship 2P fully-unleashed mandate: **ANY** SRA verdict involving REJECT or REPLACEMENT MUST be cross-model-verified via real GPT-5.5 codex T1 BEFORE any commit. Sonnet stand-in agent does RESEARCH (D1-D10 probe outputs); codex GPT-5.5 does VERIFICATION (does the SRA verdict actually hold?).
187:
188:Failure of this mandate produces over-applied REJECTs (Wave 102 lesson — trufflehog AGPL-3.0 over-applied to CLI-binary-use).
189:
190:## Anti-patterns (from Wave 102 audit lessons)
191:
192:1. **Flat license REJECT without use-class context** — Wave 102 over-applied AGPL-3.0/SSPLv1/ELv2 REJECTs to local-runtime use. SRA D1 is use-class-precise.
193:2. **Recommend stale replacement** — Wave 102 recommended KuzuDB v0.11.3 (Oct 2025) as FalkorDB replacement when FalkorDB is more current. SRA D2+D10 prevents this.
194:3. **NO LICENSE FILE = REJECT without verifying named-author intent** — Wave 102 flagged forrestchang/andrej-karpathy-skills as authority-risk. SRA D4 distinguishes named-T2 with README-stated license (acceptable) vs unknown anonymous (REJECT).
195:4. **Star-low = DOWNGRADE without industry-adoption probe** — Wave 102 flagged 201★ cnighswonger as caveat. SRA D3 + D5 + D8 disambiguate "popular but stale" from "low-star but active and adopted".
196:5. **Skip cross-model T1 on audit methodology** — Wave 102 audit was Sonnet stand-in only; never cross-verified. Ship 2X mandates T1 e2e on SRA verdicts.
197:6. **No replacement-freshness gate** — Wave 102 recommended replacements without verifying replacement is more current. D2 + D10 codify this.
198:7. **Apply stricter license standard than Anthropic itself** — Wave 102 didn't probe D7. If Anthropic ships under license X, license X is acceptable.

the default prompt leaves under-specified.

The only hardening caveat is that shell-form `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/...` relies on shell tokenization; current eee paths are safe, but exec-form `args` would be more robust if upstream changes are later accepted.
